to numeric value.\n   252→func parsePercentage(v string) (int64, error) {\n   253→\tif !strings.HasSuffix(v, \"%\") {\n   254→\t\treturn 0, fmt.Errorf(\"percentage expected, got '%s'\", v)\n   255→\t}\n   256→\tpercentage, err := strconv.ParseInt(strings.TrimRight(v, \"%\"), 10, 0)\n   257→\tif err != nil {\n   258→\t\treturn 0, fmt.Errorf(\"invalid number in percentage '%s'\", v)\n   259→\t}\n   260→\tif percentage < 0 || percentage > 100 {\n   261→\t\treturn 0, fmt.Errorf(\"percentage must be between 0 and 100\")\n   262→\t}\n   263→\treturn percentage, nil\n   264→}\n   265→\n   266→// ParseQOSReserved parses the --qos-reserved option\n   267→func ParseQOSReserved(m map[string]string) (*map[v1.ResourceName]int64, error) {\n   268→\treservations := make(map[v1.ResourceName]int64)\n   269→\tfor k, v := range m {\n   270→\t\tswitch v1.ResourceName(k) {\n   271→\t\t// Only memory resources are supported.\n   272→\t\tcase v1.ResourceMemory:\n   273→\t\t\tq, err := parsePercentage(v)\n   274→\t\t\tif err != nil {\n   275→\t\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to parse percentage %q for %q resource: %w\", v, k, err)\n   276→\t\t\t}\n   277→\t\t\treservations[v1.ResourceName(k)] = q\n   278→\t\tdefault:\n   279→\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"cannot reserve %q resource\", k)\n   280→\t\t}\n   281→\t}\n   282→\treturn &reservations, {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01VCN36WwreSAn7phMPauXT4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_0125P6DRyUdXZz1Zakx7anxe","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Kubelet Container Manager Architecture Guide\n\n## Overview\n\nThe Kubelet Container Manager (`pkg/kubelet/cm`) is the central subsystem responsible for managing container resource constraints, allocation policies, and lifecycle integration on a Kubernetes node. It enforces quality-of-service (QoS) guarantees, manages cgroups hierarchies, coordinates resource assignments across CPU, memory, and device plugins, and ensures that pods and containers respect their requested and limit guarantees.\n\nThe container manager is initialized at kubelet startup (`pkg/kubelet/kubelet.go:316`) and serves as the authoritative interface for:\n- Creating and destroying pod-level resource containers\n- Enforcing node allocatable reservations\n- Allocating exclusive CPU cores and NUMA-local memory\n- Integrating device plugins and dynamic resource allocation\n- Providing resource visibility through the Pod Resources API\n\n## Subsystem Boundaries and Dependencies\n\n### Upstream Dependencies\n\n**Kubelet Core** (`pkg/kubelet/kubelet.go`):\n- Invokes `ContainerManager.Start()` during kubelet initialization (line 793)\n- Calls `NewPodContainerManager()` when syncing pods (line 2052, 2400)\n- Uses `GetResources()` to fetch device and CDI allocations before container creation\n- Queries `GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute()` to inform scheduler about available capacity\n\n**Runtime Service** (CRI):\n- Container manager queries runtime to build initial container map during Start\n- Passes runtime service to CPU and memory managers for resource reconciliation\n- Resource managers call `UpdateContainerResources()` on runtime to apply CPU quotas\n\n**cAdvisor** (`pkg/kubelet/cadvisor`):\n- Provides machine info (CPU topology, memory capacity) at container manager initialization\n- Supplies RootFS info for local storage capacity isolation\n- Used to populate node capacity and internal capacity fields\n\n**Config Sources**:\n- Receives `config.SourcesReady` to delay resource manager operations until kubelet has synchronized pod sources\n\n### Downstream Consumers\n\n**Pod Admission Chain**:\n- Topology manager acts as a `PodAdmitHandler` returned by `GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()` (line 761)\n- Device manager pre-allocates resources during admission\n- DRA manager claims resources from resource drivers\n\n**Status Module**:\n- Updates pod status with allocated resources via `UpdateAllocatedResourcesStatus()`\n- Used by admission handlers to ensure consistency\n\n**Eviction Manager**:\n- Receives hard eviction thresholds in `NodeAllocatableConfig` (line 211)\n- Container manager computes reservation from thresholds for capacity calculations\n\n**Pod Resources Service API** (gRPC):\n- Container manager implements `CPUsProvider`, `DevicesProvider`, `MemoryProvider`, and `DynamicResourcesProvider` interfaces\n- External clients query allocated resources, device assignments, and NUMA affinity\n\n## Key Components and Responsibilities\n\n### ContainerManager Interface (container_manager.go:66)\n\nThe core abstraction defines the contract for all container management operations:\n\n**Lifecycle Management**:\n- `Start()`: Initializes all resource managers, creates cgroup hierarchies, and starts background reconciliation loops\n- `NewPodContainerManager()`: Factory method returning pod-scoped cgroup manager (either functional or noop based on configuration)\n- `InternalContainerLifecycle()`: Returns hook interface for pre/post container creation resource setup\n\n**Capacity Tracking**:\n- `GetCapacity()`: Returns total node capacity for all resource types (CPU, memory, ephemeral storage, PIDs)\n- `GetNodeAllocatableReservation()`: Returns kube-reserved and system-reserved amounts subtracted from capacity\n- `GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute()`: Returns hard enforcement limit for all pods combined\n- `GetDevicePluginResourceCapacity()`: Exposes device plugin resource availability and health status\n\n**QoS and Cgroup Hierarchy**:\n- `GetQOSContainersInfo()`: Returns paths to guaranteed, burstable, and best-effort cgroup directories\n- `UpdateQOSCgroups()`: Periodically reconciles QoS container limits based on active pod count\n- `GetPodCgroupRoot()`: Returns cgroupfs path containing all pod containers\n\n**Plugin Integration**:\n- `GetPluginRegistrationHandlers()`: Returns handlers for device plugin and DRA plugin lifecycle events\n- `GetHealthCheckers()`: Returns watchdog health checks for critical plugins\n- `UpdatePluginResources()`: Allows device plugins to update scheduler node info\n\n**Dynamic Resources**:\n- `PrepareDynamicResources()`: Invokes DRA resource driver prepare operations\n- `UnprepareDynamicResources()`: Cleans up DRA allocations on pod deletion\n- `PodMightNeedToUnprepareResources()`: Optimization hint for pod cleanup\n\n### containerManagerImpl (container_manager_linux.go:100)\n\nThe Linux implementation embeds `NodeConfig` and coordinates five major subsystem managers:\n\n**Cgroup Management** (cgroupManager):\n- Implements the `CgroupManager` interface for creating, destroying, and updating cgroups\n- Supports both cgroupfs and systemd drivers with automatic name conversion\n- Handles both cgroup v1 and v2 with appropriate resource parameter mappings\n- Methods: `Create()`, `Destroy()`, `Update()`, `Validate()`, `MemoryUsage()`, `GetCgroupConfig()`, `SetCgroupConfig()`\n\n**QoS Container Management** (qosContainerManager):\n- Maintains three top-level cgroups: guaranteed (no limits), burstable (soft limits), best-effort (background)\n- `Start()` creates initial hierarchy if `--cgroups-per-qos` is enabled\n- `UpdateCgroups()` runs periodically (line 45: every 1 minute) to adjust limits based on active pod distribution\n- Uses `qosReserved` configuration to reserve memory for memory QoS enforcement (cgroup v2 only)\n\n**CPU Manager** (cpuManager):\n- Implements static and none policies for CPU allocation\n- Static policy: reserves exclusive CPUs for guaranteed pods, shares remaining pool\n- Performs CPU affinity enforcement and topology-aware allocation\n- Reconciles container CPU settings via `UpdateContainerResources()` during periodic sync\n\n**Memory Manager** (memoryManager):\n- Implements static and best-effort policies for memory affinity\n- Static policy: pins container memory to specific NUMA nodes matching CPU allocation\n- Handles huge page allocation and memory reservation\n- Enforces `memory.min` and `memory.max` on cgroup v2\n\n**Topology Manager** (topologyManager):\n- Acts as admission-time coordinator for all resource managers\n- Collects topology hints from CPU, memory, and device managers\n- Enforces NUMA alignment policies: single-numa-node, restricted, best-effort, none\n- Scopes decisions to pod-level or container-level granularity\n\n**Device Manager** (deviceManager):\n- Manages device plugin lifecycle and resource allocation\n- Pre-allocates device IDs during pod admission\n- Provides mounted devices, environment variables, and mount points at container start\n- Tracks unhealthy devices and reports capacity changes\n\n**DRA Manager** (draManager):\n- Implements Dynamic Resource Allocation (KEP-3063) for custom resources\n- Manages resource driver communication and claim provisioning\n- Handles resource health monitoring and updates\n- Feature-gated: `DynamicResourceAllocation` feature flag\n\n### Pod Container Manager (pod_container_manager_linux.go:40)\n\nManages pod-level cgroup hierarchy when QoS cgroup feature is enabled:\n\n```\nkubepods/\n├── burstable/\n│   └── pod<uid>/\n│       ├── container1\n│       └── container2\n└── best-effort/\n    └── pod<uid>/\n        └── container1\n```\n\n**Key Methods**:\n- `EnsureExists()`: Creates or validates pod cgroup existence, computing QoS-specific limits (lines 74-100)\n- `GetPodContainerName()`: Converts pod to internal cgroup path representation\n- `Destroy()`: Removes pod cgroup and all children\n- `GetAllPodsFromCgroups()`: Reconciles current cgroup state with active pod set\n- `GetPodCgroupMemoryUsage()`: Queries memory.usage_in_bytes for entire pod\n\n**Feature Integration** (lines 79-86):\n- Disables CPU CFS quota for exclusive-CPU pods via `DisableCPUQuotaWithExclusiveCPUs`\n- Enables memory QoS (memory.high) for burstable pods on cgroup v2\n\n### Internal Container Lifecycle (internal_container_lifecycle.go:28)\n\nHook interface called at critical container lifecycle points:\n\n**PreCreateContainer** (internal_container_lifecycle_linux.go:31):\n- CPU manager sets `CpusetCpus` container config if exclusive CPUs allocated\n- Memory manager sets `CpusetMems` for NUMA affinity\n- Called by kubelet before CRI CreateContainer RPC\n\n**PreStartContainer** (internal_container_lifecycle.go:41):\n- CPU manager records container ID → pod UID + container name mapping\n- Memory manager records container ID for future queries\n- Topology manager records resource hint decisions for post-mortem analysis\n\n**PostStopContainer** (internal_container_lifecycle.go:55):\n- Topology manager removes container entries from hint cache\n- CPU/memory managers clean up only on full `RemoveContainer()` call from pod worker\n\n### QoS Container Manager (qos_container_manager_linux.go:54)\n\nImplements periodic resource reconciliation for QoS containers:\n\n**Start Sequence** (lines 84-115):\n1. Validates cgroup root exists\n2. Creates guaranteed (no explicit cgroup), burstable, and best-effort top-level containers\n3. Initializes with limits based on node allocatable minus reserved resources\n\n**UpdateCgroups Logic** (called every minute from containerManagerImpl Start, line 695):\n- Queries active pods to compute QoS class distribution\n- Calculates per-pod share of QoS container limits (e.g., 100m CPU / 10 burstable pods = 10m per pod)\n- Updates cgroup limits via `cgroupManager.Update()` to enforce soft boundaries\n- Prevents CPU starvation when unbalanced pod counts exist\n\n**QoS Reserved** (qos_container_manager_linux.go:62):\n- Configurable percentage of memory reserved for memory QoS enforcement\n- Only meaningful on cgroup v2 where `memory.min` provides hard floor\n- Default: 0% (no explicit reservation)\n\n### Cgroup Manager (cgroup_manager_linux.go)\n\nPlatform abstraction layer for cgroup creation and updates:\n\n**Driver Implementations**:\n- **cgroupfs driver**: Direct filesystem manipulation of `/sys/fs/cgroup/*`\n- **systemd driver**: Delegates to systemd via `org.freedesktop.systemd1` D-Bus interface\n- Automatic driver selection based on `--cgroup-driver` kubelet flag\n\n**Name Conversion** (lines 56-82):\n- Internal: `[\"kubepods\", \"burstable\", \"pod1234-abcd\"]` (CgroupName)\n- Filesystem (cgroupfs): `/kubepods/burstable/pod1234-abcd`\n- Systemd: `/kubepods.slice/kubepods-burstable.slice/kubepods-burstable-pod1234_abcd.slice`\n- Automatic remapping: `-` becomes `_` in systemd names to preserve reversibility\n\n**Cgroup V1 vs V2** (cgroup_v1_manager_linux.go, cgroup_v2_manager_linux.go):\n- V1: Separate controllers per resource (cpu, memory, cpuset, etc.)\n- V2: Unified single hierarchy with resource types as file prefixes\n- Manager version returned by `Version()`: 1 or 2\n- Different resource parameter mappings (e.g., `cpu.shares` vs `cpu.weight`)\n\n**Resource Config** (types.go:27):\n- Encapsulates CPU (shares, quota, period, cpuset), memory, hugetlb limits, and PID limits\n- Applied uniformly across both cgroup versions via abstraction layer\n\n## End-to-End Control Flow\n\n### Initialization Flow (container_manager_linux.go:207-391)\n\n```\nNewContainerManager()\n├─ GetCgroupSubsystems()                           # Detect mounted cgroup subsystems\n├─ DetectCgroupVersion()                           # v1 vs v2\n├─ GetCadvisorMachineInfo()                        # CPU topology, total capacity\n├─ NewCgroupManager()                              # Create driver (cgroupfs or systemd)\n├─ NewQOSContainerManager()                        # Initialize QoS coordinator\n├─ topologymanager.NewManager()                    # Create NUMA alignment policy\n├─ devicemanager.NewManagerImpl()                   # Initialize device plugin manager\n├─ cpumanager.NewManager()                         # Initialize CPU allocation policy\n├─ memorymanager.NewManager()                      # Initialize memory affinity policy\n└─ Spawn fan-in goroutines for resource updates    # Merge device/DRA update channels\n```\n\nKey decisions made here:\n- **Cgroup driver selection**: cgroupfs vs systemd (line 255)\n- **QoS hierarchy enabled**: if `--cgroups-per-qos=true` (line 398)\n- **CPU policy**: none, static, distribution-specific (line 328)\n- **Memory policy**: none, static, best-effort (line 344)\n- **Topology scope**: pod or container (line 299)\n\n### Start Flow (container_manager_linux.go:615-710)\n\nCalled by kubelet during sync loop initialization:\n\n```\nStart(ctx, node, activePods, getNode, sourcesReady, podStatusProvider, runtimeService, localStorageCapacityIsolation)\n├─ buildContainerMapAndRunningSetFromRuntime()    # Query current running containers via CRI\n├─ draManager.Start()                              # Initialize DRA resource claims\n├─ cpuManager.Start()                              # Load CPU allocation state from disk\n│   └─ reconcileState()                            # Periodically reconcile CPU allocations with cgroups\n├─ memoryManager.Start()                           # Load memory affinity state from disk\n├─ validateNodeAllocatable()                       # Verify capacity > allocatable\n├─ setupNode()                                     # Create cgroup hierarchy and system containers\n└─ deviceManager.Start()                           # Begin watching device plugins\n```\n\n**setupNode Details** (lines 504-584):\n1. Validate CPU hardcapping available (CPU CFS quota support)\n2. Setup kernel tunables if `--protect-kernel-defaults` not set\n3. Create node allocatable cgroup root: `kubepods/kubepods.slice`\n4. Start QoS container manager to create top-level QoS containers\n5. Enforce node allocatable limits by setting cgroup max resources\n6. Create system and kubelet cgroups for non-containerized processes\n7. Spawn background goroutines for periodic tasks (ensure state functions, QoS updates)\n\n### Pod Admission Flow\n\nContainer manager participates via topology manager returned by `GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()`:\n\n```\nkubelet AdmitPod()\n├─ topologyManager.Admit()                         # Collect topology hints\n│   ├─ cpuManager.GetTopologyHints()               # Preferred NUMA nodes for CPU\n│   ├─ memoryManager.GetTopologyHints()            # Preferred NUMA nodes for memory\n│   └─ deviceManager.GetTopologyHints()            # Device topology\n├─ deviceManager.Allocate()                        # Reserve device IDs\n├─ draManager.Prepare()                            # Claim resources from drivers\n└─ [admit decision: ALLOW / DENY]\n```\n\nIf admission succeeds:\n- CPU manager reserves exclusive CPUs\n- Device manager marks device IDs as allocated\n- DRA manager stores resource claims in state\n\n### Pod Sync Flow\n\nFor each pod reaching `Sync` phase:\n\n```\nkubelet SyncPod()\n├─ podContainerManager.EnsureExists()              # Create pod-level cgroup if needed\n├─ [for each container]\n│   ├─ internalContainerLifecycle.PreCreateContainer()\n│   │   ├─ cpuManager.GetCPUAffinity()             # Retrieve exclusive CPUs\n│   │   └─ memoryManager.GetMemoryNUMANodes()      # Retrieve NUMA affinity\n│   ├─ CreateContainer(containerConfig)            # CRI call with cpuset_cpus, cpuset_mems\n│   └─ internalContainerLifecycle.PreStartContainer()\n│       ├─ cpuManager.AddContainer()               # Record containerID mapping\n│       └─ memoryManager.AddContainer()            # Record containerID mapping\n└─ [periodic reconciliation]\n    └─ cpuManager reconcileState()                 # Verify cpuset_cpus matches cgroups\n        └─ runtimeService.UpdateContainerResources() # Correct drift\n```\n\n### Periodic Maintenance\n\n**Every 1 minute** (line 695):\n- Execute `ensureStateFunc` for system/kubelet cgroups to maintain process placement\n- Call `qosContainerManager.UpdateCgroups()` to rebalance QoS limits\n\n**Every 5 minutes** (line 695):\n- CPU manager reconciliation (if enabled)\n- Execute custom periodic tasks\n\n## Cgroup Hierarchy and Resource Enforcement\n\n### Hierarchy Structure (Linux with --cgroups-per-qos)\n\n```\n├─ systemd driver: /\n│  └─ kubepods.slice\n│     ├─ kubepods-kubepods.slice (node allocatable enforcement)\n│     │  ├─ kubepods-kubepods-burstable.slice (QoS class)\n│     │  │  └─ kubepods-kubepods-burstable-pod<uid>.slice (pod)\n│     │  │     ├─ container-id (container)\n│     │  │     └─ container-id\n│     │  └─ kubepods-kubepods-besteffort.slice (QoS class)\n│     │     └─ kubepods-kubepods-besteffort-pod<uid>.slice (pod)\n│     └─ (guaranteed pods placed directly in kubepods.slice/pod<uid>/)\n│\n├─ cgroupfs driver: /kubepods/\n│  ├─ kubepods (node allocatable enforcement)\n│  │  ├─ burstable/ (QoS class)\n│  │  │  └─ pod<uid>/ (pod)\n│  │  │     ├─ container-id (container)\n│  │  │     └─ container-id\n│  │  └─ besteffort/ (QoS class)\n│  │     └─ pod<uid>/ (pod)\n│  └─ (guaranteed pods in kubepods/pod<uid>/)\n```\n\n### Resource Parameter Mapping\n\n**CPU Limits** (cpu_manager.go):\n- Exclusive allocation: `cpu.cpuset` to specific cores\n- Shared allocation: `cpu.shares` (relative weight) + optional `cpu.cfs_quota_us`\n- Static policy: sum of exclusive allocations subtracted from shared pool\n\n**Memory Limits** (memory_manager.go):\n- Cgroup v1: `memory.limit_in_bytes` enforced hard limit\n- Cgroup v2:\n  - `memory.max`: hard limit (OOM at exceeding)\n  - `memory.min`: soft guarantee (will not reclaim below this)\n  - `memory.high`: throttle point (memcg pressure before OOM)\n- NUMA affinity: `cpuset.mems` (not a limit, but advisory for page allocation)\n\n**PID Limits** (types.go:41):\n- `pids.max`: maximum number of processes in cgroup\n- Pod-level: `--pod-pids-limit` flag (default 4096 on cgroup v2, unlimited on v1)\n\n## Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### Failure Mode: Cgroup Hierarchy Not Available\n\n**Symptom**: `\"failed to get mounted cgroup subsystems\"`\n\n**Root Cause**: Required cgroup subsystems not mounted in `/sys/fs/cgroup`\n\n**Handling** (lines 210-213):\n- Container manager init fails, kubelet enters crash loop\n- No graceful degradation; cgroups are mandatory for proper resource isolation\n\n**Mitigation**: Ensure cgroup controllers mounted before kubelet starts (typically systemd handles this)\n\n### Failure Mode: CPU Hardcapping Unavailable\n\n**Symptom**: `\"CPU hardcapping unsupported\"` (soft requirement, stored in Status.SoftRequirements)\n\n**Root Cause**: CPU CFS period/quota files missing (older kernel < 2.6.24 or cgroup v1 broken)\n\n**Handling** (lines 511-513):\n- Kubelet continues but cannot enforce CPU limits\n- CPU shares still applied, but no hard quota ceiling\n- Scheduled workloads may oversubscribe CPU\n\n**Mitigation**: Use kernel 5.x+ with full cgroup support enabled\n\n### Failure Mode: Cgroup Root Path Invalid\n\n**Symptom**: `\"invalid configuration: cgroups-per-qos was specified and cgroup-root was not specified\"`\n\n**Root Cause**: `--cgroup-root` flag missing when `--cgroups-per-qos=true`\n\n**Handling** (lines 260-261):\n- Init fails; kubelet cannot place pods\n- Required configuration: must specify `--cgroup-root=/kubepods` (or similar)\n\n### Failure Mode: Device Plugin Allocation Stale\n\n**Symptom**: Container fails to start; device IDs don't correspond to actual devices\n\n**Root Cause**: Device plugin crashed; allocation state became inconsistent\n\n**Handling** (devicemanager):\n- Pre-allocated device IDs may not exist at container start\n- Kubelet calls plugin's `GetDeviceRunContainerOptions()` which validates availability\n- If plugin returns error or device unavailable, container creation fails\n- Pod enters failed state; eviction or manual recovery required\n\n**Mitigation**: Device plugins must be highly available; use checkpoint/recovery\n\n### Failure Mode: Resource Reservation Exceeds Capacity\n\n**Symptom**: `\"node allocatable is negative\"`\n\n**Root Cause**: `--system-reserved` + `--kube-reserved` > node capacity\n\n**Handling** (kubelet node init, called by container manager):\n- Validation fails; kubelet cannot start\n- Must reconfigure reservations to be less than total capacity\n\n### Limit: Maximum Concurrent Resource Allocations\n\n**Constraint**: Single goroutine in `reconcileState()` per resource manager\n\n**Impact**: CPU and memory manager reconciliation runs serially, not in parallel\n\n**Rationale**: Simplifies state consistency; avoids thundering herd on runtime updates\n\n**Mitigation**: None; inherent architectural choice for safety\n\n### Limit: Cgroup Name Length\n\n**Constraint**: 64-character limit for cgroup filesystem names (kernel limit)\n\n**Impact**: Very long pod UIDs or container names may exceed directory name limits\n\n**Workaround**: Pod UID encoded as `pod<hash>` to reduce length\n\n### Design Tradeoff: State Persistence\n\n**Choice**: Separate state files per resource manager (cpumanager, memorymanager)\n\n**Tradeoff**:\n- ✅ Fault isolation: device manager crash doesn't corrupt CPU state\n- ✅ Per-manager recovery: CPU manager can replay allocations independently\n- ❌ Distributed consistency: no global transaction across all managers\n- ❌ Orphaned state: if pod UID reused, old allocations may not be cleaned (unlikely but possible)\n\n**Consistency Mechanism**: Active pod reconciliation on startup\n- Container manager queries runtime for all running containers\n- Pod controller ensures stale pod objects deleted before UIDs reused\n- Kubelet sync loop continuously validates pod ↔ cgroup mappings\n\n### Design Tradeoff: QoS Limits Soft-Enforced\n\n**Choice**: QoS container limits updated every 1 minute, not real-time\n\n**Rationale** (qos_container_manager_linux.go:45):\n- Real-time updates on pod add/remove would cause cgroup churn\n- 1-minute delay acceptable for capacity planning\n- Individual pod limits still enforced immediately via pod cgroups\n\n**Implication**: Transient oversubscription possible for up to 1 minute after pod deletion\n\n### Design Tradeoff: Topology Manager as Pod Admit Handler\n\n**Choice**: Topology manager admits or denies pods based on NUMA alignment hints\n\n**Rationale**:\n- Single authoritative decision point for all resource managers\n- Prevents race conditions between CPU, memory, device allocation decisions\n- Container must be started if admitted; no subsequent allocation failures expected\n\n**Risk**: If topology manager approves but CPU/device managers later fail to deliver:\n- Container creation fails; pod enters failed state\n- Should not happen with proper implementation; indicates bug\n\n## Extension Points, Customization Hooks, and Associated Risks\n\n### 1. CPU Manager Policy (cpu_manager.go:30-50)\n\n**Extension Points**:\n- Policy interface: `Manager` with `Allocate()`, `AddContainer()`, `RemoveContainer()`\n- Pluggable policies: `none`, `static`, distribution-specific (e.g., Intel platform extensions)\n\n**Customization**:\n```go\n// Implement interface in pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/\ntype CustomPolicy struct {\n    // ...\n}\nfunc (p *CustomPolicy) Allocate(pod, container) error { ... }\n```\n\nRegister in `cpu_manager.go` `NewManager()` switch statement.\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Incorrect exclusive CPU reservation: CPUs allocated to multiple containers (security/isolation issue)\n- ❌ Unbounded latency in `Allocate()`: Blocks pod admission; slow policies starve cluster\n- ❌ Inconsistent state: Custom policy doesn't persist state to disk; allocations lost on kubelet restart\n\n### 2. Memory Manager Policy (memory_manager.go:30-50)\n\n**Extension Points**:\n- Policy interface: `Manager` with `Allocate()`, `GetMemoryNUMANodes()`\n- Pluggable policies: `none`, `static`, `best-effort`\n\n**Risk**: Similar to CPU policy; mismatch between pod memory limit and allocated NUMA nodes causes page allocation contention\n\n### 3. Topology Manager Policy (topologymanager/policy.go)\n\n**Extension Points**:\n- Hint aggregation policies: `none`, `best-effort`, `restricted`, `single-numa-node`\n- Custom hint providers: `HintProvider` interface\n\n**Customization**:\n```go\ntype CustomProvider struct{}\nfunc (p *CustomProvider) GetTopologyHints(pod, container) map[string][]TopologyHint { ... }\nfunc (p *CustomProvider) GetPodTopologyHints(pod) map[string][]TopologyHint { ... }\n\ncm.topologyManager.AddHintProvider(logger, customProvider)\n```\n\n**Risk**: ❌ Custom providers returning inconsistent hints can cause deadlock (all providers reject; admission fails despite available resources)\n\n### 4. Device Plugin Interface (devicemanager/plugin/)\n\n**Extension Point**: External device plugin binary communicating via Unix socket gRPC\n\n**Plugin Lifecycle**:\n1. Plugin registers itself via inotify watch on `/var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins/`\n2. Kubelet calls plugin's `ListAndWatch()` to get available devices\n3. On pod admission: `Allocate()` called to reserve devices\n4. At container start: `GetDeviceRunContainerOptions()` called for mounts/env vars\n5. On pod deletion: device IDs freed; no explicit plugin call\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Plugin crash: Allocated devices become inaccessible; pod hangs at container start\n- ❌ Plugin stale socket: Old plugin process left running; new plugin can't claim socket\n- ❌ Unbounded device allocation: Plugin allocates more than reported capacity; node oversubscribed\n- ❌ Health check bypass: Plugin reports healthy but devices fail; kubelet doesn't know to evict\n\n**Mitigation**:\n- Plugin checkpoint/recovery mechanism\n- Kubelet kubelet health checks plugins via `GetHealthStatus()`\n- Device plugin spec forbids over-allocation\n\n### 5. Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA) Manager (dra/)\n\n**Extension Point**: External resource driver plugin with similar lifecycle to device plugins\n\n**Customization**:\n- Drivers implement `DynamicResourceAllocation` controller interface\n- Kubelet DRA manager calls driver via gRPC\n- Resource claims stored in cluster; recovered on pod restart\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Driver unavailable: Pending claims never provisioned; pod waits indefinitely\n- ❌ Claim conflicts: Two pods claim same resource; inconsistent state\n- ❌ Resource leak: Claim orphaned if cluster API unreachable; resource wasted\n\n**Mitigation**:\n- Claim state reconciliation against cluster state\n- Timeout-based claim cleanup\n\n### 6. Cgroup Driver Selection (cgroup_manager_linux.go:255)\n\n**Extension Point**: `--cgroup-driver` kubelet flag: `cgroupfs` or `systemd`\n\n**Customization**: Unlikely to extend; choice is platform-dependent\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Driver mismatch: Kubelet uses cgroupfs but container runtime uses systemd; cgroup limits don't apply\n- ❌ Name conversion bugs: Systemd cgroup path has invalid characters; cgroup not created\n\n### 7. QoS Reserved Configuration (container_manager.go:189)\n\n**Extension Point**: `--qos-reserved` kubelet flag (e.g., `memory=5%`)\n\n**Customization**: Percentage of memory reserved for memory QoS enforcement (cgroup v2 only)\n\n**Risk**: ❌ Over-reservation: QoS reserved memory wasted; pods can't use capacity\n- ❌ Under-reservation: Memory QoS enforcement fails; burstable pods get OOMed\n\n### 8. Node Allocatable Configuration\n\n**Extension Point**: `--system-reserved`, `--kube-reserved`, `--enforce-node-allocatable` flags\n\n**Customization**:\n```\n--system-reserved=cpu=100m,memory=512Mi,ephemeral-storage=1Gi\n--kube-reserved=cpu=100m,memory=100Mi\n--enforce-node-allocatable=pods,kube-reserved,system-reserved\n```\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Insufficient reservation: System OOMs or crashes due to pod memory pressure\n- ❌ Excessive reservation: Cluster capacity wasted; scheduler creates pod that can't fit in allocatable\n\n## Operational Implications\n\n### Performance Considerations\n\n**CPU Impact**:\n- Static CPU policy: O(n) CPU mask operations on pod add/remove (n = number of CPUs)\n- Topology manager: O(n*m) hint aggregation (n = managers, m = pods per node)\n- Cgroup updates: ~1ms per cgroup on cgroupfs; ~10ms via systemd D-Bus\n\n**Memory Impact**:\n- Container manager holds ~1KB per pod + per-container state\n- CPU manager state file grows with pod count (O(n) JSON objects)\n- Large pod counts (>1000 pods/node) require periodic state file compaction\n\n**Disk I/O**:\n- State persistence: CPU/memory managers write to `/var/lib/kubelet/` every allocation\n- Frequent pod churn (e.g., batch jobs) causes high I/O; consider SSD for kubelet dir\n\n### Reliability Considerations\n\n**Pod Reliability**:\n- Guaranteed pods: CPU and memory limits enforced; no eviction due to overload\n- Burstable pods: Soft limits; throttled but not OOMed unless node-level memory pressure\n- Best-effort pods: No resource guarantees; first to be evicted if node overloaded\n\n**Node Reliability**:\n- Cgroup enforcement essential for kubelet/system stability\n- Failure to enforce leads to system OOM; node becomes unresponsive\n- Device plugin crashes: Critical if pods depend on devices; manual intervention required\n- DRA resource driver: Failure blocks new pod admission until recovered\n\n**Graceful Degradation**:\n- CPU hardcapping unavailable: Kubelet continues but won't enforce quotas\n- Device plugin unavailable: New device allocations blocked; existing pods unaffected\n- DRA driver unavailable: Pending resource claims never provisioned; pod waits\n\n### Observability\n\n**Key Metrics** (pkg/kubelet/metrics/):\n- `kubelet_node_config_*`: Node capacity, allocatable, reserved\n- `container_*`: cAdvisor metrics for CPU, memory, I/O per container\n- `kubelet_pod_resource_status`: Allocated resources per pod (gRPC API)\n\n**Debugging**:\n- Check pod cgroup path: `kubelet logs | grep \"pod.*cgroup\"`\n- Query pod cgroup limits: `cat /sys/fs/cgroup/pods/pod<uid>/cpu.max`\n- Trace resource admission: Enable `kube-apiserver --vmodule=admission=4`\n- Monitor device plugin: Check `/var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins/<plugin>.sock`\n\n**Alerts**:\n- Node allocatable exceeded: CPU or memory reservation math wrong\n- Cgroup creation failures: Filesystem full or permission denied\n- Device allocation stale: Device plugin crashed; manual recovery needed\n- QoS limit churn: Frequent updates indicate high pod churn; consider batch job consolidation\n\n### Maintenance and Upgrades\n\n**State Cleanup**:\n- Stale state files: `/var/lib/kubelet/cpu_manager_state` removed on `--force-reset` flags\n- Orphaned cgroups: Manual cleanup if containers crash; `cgdelete` utility required\n\n**Cgroup Version Transitions**:\n- Upgrade from v1 to v2: Requires kernel with unified hierarchy support (5.2+)\n- No automatic migration; pods must be drained before cgroup root remount\n- Different resource parameter names (e.g., `cpu.weight` in v2 vs `cpu.shares` in v1)\n\n**Device Plugin Updates**:\n- Plugins should be deployed via DaemonSet with graceful shutdown hooks\n- Old plugin socket cleaned up before new plugin registered\n\n## Important Files and Directory Map\n\n### Core Container Manager\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/lifecycle/` | Pod admission handlers; topology manager participates |\n| `pkg/kubelet/container/` | CRI abstraction; calls to update container resources |\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Kubelet Container Manager subsystem provides a unified abstraction for resource isolation and allocation across multiple dimensions: CPU exclusivity, memory NUMA affinity, device plugins, and dynamic resource drivers. By coordinating five semi-independent resource managers through a topology-aware admission handler, it enforces node allocatable constraints while supporting sophisticated allocation strategies.\n\nThe subsystem is well-architected for Linux cgroups but exhibits tight coupling to cgroup mechanics; porting to other isolation mechanisms (e.g., Windows Job Objects) requires significant reengineering. State management across managers is eventually consistent, relying on periodic reconciliation rather than global transactions. Operational success depends heavily on proper cgroup configuration, timely device plugin recovery, and correct reservation math to prevent node-level resource starvation.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":11,"cache_creation_input_tokens":751,"cache_read_input_tokens":56636,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":751},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4b7949b9-c411-477c-9c69-daf3270d81a2","uuid":"218a49d7-2a7a-4795-bed1-3d071b07bbe1"}

It enforces quality-of-service (QoS) guarantees, manages cgroups hierarchies, coordinates resource assignments across CPU, memory, and device plugins, and ensures that pods and containers respect their requested and limit guarantees.\n\nThe container manager is initialized at kubelet startup (`pkg/kubelet/kubelet.go:316`) and serves as the authoritative interface for:\n- Creating and destroying pod-level resource containers\n- Enforcing node allocatable reservations\n- Allocating exclusive CPU cores and NUMA-local memory\n- Integrating device plugins and dynamic resource allocation\n- Providing resource visibility through the Pod Resources API\n\n## Subsystem Boundaries and Dependencies\n\n### Upstream Dependencies\n\n**Kubelet Core** (`pkg/kubelet/kubelet.go`):\n- Invokes `ContainerManager.Start()` during kubelet initialization (line 793)\n- Calls `NewPodContainerManager()` when syncing pods (line 2052, 2400)\n- Uses `GetResources()` to fetch device and CDI allocations before container creation\n- Queries `GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute()` to inform scheduler about available capacity\n\n**Runtime Service** (CRI):\n- Container manager queries runtime to build initial container map during Start\n- Passes runtime service to CPU and memory managers for resource reconciliation\n- Resource managers call `UpdateContainerResources()` on runtime to apply CPU quotas\n\n**cAdvisor** (`pkg/kubelet/cadvisor`):\n- Provides machine info (CPU topology, memory capacity) at container manager initialization\n- Supplies RootFS info for local storage capacity isolation\n- Used to populate node capacity and internal capacity fields\n\n**Config Sources**:\n- Receives `config.SourcesReady` to delay resource manager operations until kubelet has synchronized pod sources\n\n### Downstream Consumers\n\n**Pod Admission Chain**:\n- Topology manager acts as a `PodAdmitHandler` returned by `GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()` (line 761)\n- Device manager pre-allocates resources during admission\n- DRA manager claims resources from resource drivers\n\n**Status Module**:\n- Updates pod status with allocated resources via `UpdateAllocatedResourcesStatus()`\n- Used by admission handlers to ensure consistency\n\n**Eviction Manager**:\n- Receives hard eviction thresholds in `NodeAllocatableConfig` (line 211)\n- Container manager computes reservation from thresholds for capacity calculations\n\n**Pod Resources Service API** (gRPC):\n- Container manager implements `CPUsProvider`, `DevicesProvider`, `MemoryProvider`, and `DynamicResourcesProvider` interfaces\n- External clients query allocated resources, device assignments, and NUMA affinity\n\n## Key Components and Responsibilities\n\n### ContainerManager Interface (container_manager.go:66)\n\nThe core abstraction defines the contract for all container management operations:\n\n**Lifecycle Management**:\n- `Start()`: Initializes all resource managers, creates cgroup hierarchies, and starts background reconciliation loops\n- `NewPodContainerManager()`: Factory method returning pod-scoped cgroup manager (either functional or noop based on configuration)\n- `InternalContainerLifecycle()`: Returns hook interface for pre/post container creation resource setup\n\n**Capacity Tracking**:\n- `GetCapacity()`: Returns total node capacity for all resource types (CPU, memory, ephemeral storage, PIDs)\n- `GetNodeAllocatableReservation()`: Returns kube-reserved and system-reserved amounts subtracted from capacity\n- `GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute()`: Returns hard enforcement limit for all pods combined\n- `GetDevicePluginResourceCapacity()`: Exposes device plugin resource availability and health status\n\n**QoS and Cgroup Hierarchy**:\n- `GetQOSContainersInfo()`: Returns paths to guaranteed, burstable, and best-effort cgroup directories\n- `UpdateQOSCgroups()`: Periodically reconciles QoS container limits based on active pod count\n- `GetPodCgroupRoot()`: Returns cgroupfs path containing all pod containers\n\n**Plugin Integration**:\n- `GetPluginRegistrationHandlers()`: Returns handlers for device plugin and DRA plugin lifecycle events\n- `GetHealthCheckers()`: Returns watchdog health checks for critical plugins\n- `UpdatePluginResources()`: Allows device plugins to update scheduler node info\n\n**Dynamic Resources**:\n- `PrepareDynamicResources()`: Invokes DRA resource driver prepare operations\n- `UnprepareDynamicResources()`: Cleans up DRA allocations on pod deletion\n- `PodMightNeedToUnprepareResources()`: Optimization hint for pod cleanup\n\n### containerManagerImpl (container_manager_linux.go:100)\n\nThe Linux implementation embeds `NodeConfig` and coordinates five major subsystem managers:\n\n**Cgroup Management** (cgroupManager):\n- Implements the `CgroupManager` interface for creating, destroying, and updating cgroups\n- Supports both cgroupfs and systemd drivers with automatic name conversion\n- Handles both cgroup v1 and v2 with appropriate resource parameter mappings\n- Methods: `Create()`, `Destroy()`, `Update()`, `Validate()`, `MemoryUsage()`, `GetCgroupConfig()`, `SetCgroupConfig()`\n\n**QoS Container Management** (qosContainerManager):\n- Maintains three top-level cgroups: guaranteed (no limits), burstable (soft limits), best-effort (background)\n- `Start()` creates initial hierarchy if `--cgroups-per-qos` is enabled\n- `UpdateCgroups()` runs periodically (line 45: every 1 minute) to adjust limits based on active pod distribution\n- Uses `qosReserved` configuration to reserve memory for memory QoS enforcement (cgroup v2 only)\n\n**CPU Manager** (cpuManager):\n- Implements static and none policies for CPU allocation\n- Static policy: reserves exclusive CPUs for guaranteed pods, shares remaining pool\n- Performs CPU affinity enforcement and topology-aware allocation\n- Reconciles container CPU settings via `UpdateContainerResources()` during periodic sync\n\n**Memory Manager** (memoryManager):\n- Implements static and best-effort policies for memory affinity\n- Static policy: pins container memory to specific NUMA nodes matching CPU allocation\n- Handles huge page allocation and memory reservation\n- Enforces `memory.min` and `memory.max` on cgroup v2\n\n**Topology Manager** (topologyManager):\n- Acts as admission-time coordinator for all resource managers\n- Collects topology hints from CPU, memory, and device managers\n- Enforces NUMA alignment policies: single-numa-node, restricted, best-effort, none\n- Scopes decisions to pod-level or container-level granularity\n\n**Device Manager** (deviceManager):\n- Manages device plugin lifecycle and resource allocation\n- Pre-allocates device IDs during pod admission\n- Provides mounted devices, environment variables, and mount points at container start\n- Tracks unhealthy devices and reports capacity changes\n\n**DRA Manager** (draManager):\n- Implements Dynamic Resource Allocation (KEP-3063) for custom resources\n- Manages resource driver communication and claim provisioning\n- Handles resource health monitoring and updates\n- Feature-gated: `DynamicResourceAllocation` feature flag\n\n### Pod Container Manager (pod_container_manager_linux.go:40)\n\nManages pod-level cgroup hierarchy when QoS cgroup feature is enabled:\n\n```\nkubepods/\n├── burstable/\n│   └── pod<uid>/\n│       ├── container1\n│       └── container2\n└── best-effort/\n    └── pod<uid>/\n        └── container1\n```\n\n**Key Methods**:\n- `EnsureExists()`: Creates or validates pod cgroup existence, computing QoS-specific limits (lines 74-100)\n- `GetPodContainerName()`: Converts pod to internal cgroup path representation\n- `Destroy()`: Removes pod cgroup and all children\n- `GetAllPodsFromCgroups()`: Reconciles current cgroup state with active pod set\n- `GetPodCgroupMemoryUsage()`: Queries memory.usage_in_bytes for entire pod\n\n**Feature Integration** (lines 79-86):\n- Disables CPU CFS quota for exclusive-CPU pods via `DisableCPUQuotaWithExclusiveCPUs`\n- Enables memory QoS (memory.high) for burstable pods on cgroup v2\n\n### Internal Container Lifecycle (internal_container_lifecycle.go:28)\n\nHook interface called at critical container lifecycle points:\n\n**PreCreateContainer** (internal_container_lifecycle_linux.go:31):\n- CPU manager sets `CpusetCpus` container config if exclusive CPUs allocated\n- Memory manager sets `CpusetMems` for NUMA affinity\n- Called by kubelet before CRI CreateContainer RPC\n\n**PreStartContainer** (internal_container_lifecycle.go:41):\n- CPU manager records container ID → pod UID + container name mapping\n- Memory manager records container ID for future queries\n- Topology manager records resource hint decisions for post-mortem analysis\n\n**PostStopContainer** (internal_container_lifecycle.go:55):\n- Topology manager removes container entries from hint cache\n- CPU/memory managers clean up only on full `RemoveContainer()` call from pod worker\n\n### QoS Container Manager (qos_container_manager_linux.go:54)\n\nImplements periodic resource reconciliation for QoS containers:\n\n**Start Sequence** (lines 84-115):\n1. Validates cgroup root exists\n2. Creates guaranteed (no explicit cgroup), burstable, and best-effort top-level containers\n3. Initializes with limits based on node allocatable minus reserved resources\n\n**UpdateCgroups Logic** (called every minute from containerManagerImpl Start, line 695):\n- Queries active pods to compute QoS class distribution\n- Calculates per-pod share of QoS container limits (e.g., 100m CPU / 10 burstable pods = 10m per pod)\n- Updates cgroup limits via `cgroupManager.Update()` to enforce soft boundaries\n- Prevents CPU starvation when unbalanced pod counts exist\n\n**QoS Reserved** (qos_container_manager_linux.go:62):\n- Configurable percentage of memory reserved for memory QoS enforcement\n- Only meaningful on cgroup v2 where `memory.min` provides hard floor\n- Default: 0% (no explicit reservation)\n\n### Cgroup Manager (cgroup_manager_linux.go)\n\nPlatform abstraction layer for cgroup creation and updates:\n\n**Driver Implementations**:\n- **cgroupfs driver**: Direct filesystem manipulation of `/sys/fs/cgroup/*`\n- **systemd driver**: Delegates to systemd via `org.freedesktop.systemd1` D-Bus interface\n- Automatic driver selection based on `--cgroup-driver` kubelet flag\n\n**Name Conversion** (lines 56-82):\n- Internal: `[\"kubepods\", \"burstable\", \"pod1234-abcd\"]` (CgroupName)\n- Filesystem (cgroupfs): `/kubepods/burstable/pod1234-abcd`\n- Systemd: `/kubepods.slice/kubepods-burstable.slice/kubepods-burstable-pod1234_abcd.slice`\n- Automatic remapping: `-` becomes `_` in systemd names to preserve reversibility\n\n**Cgroup V1 vs V2** (cgroup_v1_manager_linux.go, cgroup_v2_manager_linux.go):\n- V1: Separate controllers per resource (cpu, memory, cpuset, etc.)\n- V2: Unified single hierarchy with resource types as file prefixes\n- Manager version returned by `Version()`: 1 or 2\n- Different resource parameter mappings (e.g., `cpu.shares` vs `cpu.weight`)\n\n**Resource Config** (types.go:27):\n- Encapsulates CPU (shares, quota, period, cpuset), memory, hugetlb limits, and PID limits\n- Applied uniformly across both cgroup versions via abstraction layer\n\n## End-to-End Control Flow\n\n### Initialization Flow (container_manager_linux.go:207-391)\n\n```\nNewContainerManager()\n├─ GetCgroupSubsystems()                           # Detect mounted cgroup subsystems\n├─ DetectCgroupVersion()                           # v1 vs v2\n├─ GetCadvisorMachineInfo()                        # CPU topology, total capacity\n├─ NewCgroupManager()                              # Create driver (cgroupfs or systemd)\n├─ NewQOSContainerManager()                        # Initialize QoS coordinator\n├─ topologymanager.NewManager()                    # Create NUMA alignment policy\n├─ devicemanager.NewManagerImpl()                   # Initialize device plugin manager\n├─ cpumanager.NewManager()                         # Initialize CPU allocation policy\n├─ memorymanager.NewManager()                      # Initialize memory affinity policy\n└─ Spawn fan-in goroutines for resource updates    # Merge device/DRA update channels\n```\n\nKey decisions made here:\n- **Cgroup driver selection**: cgroupfs vs systemd (line 255)\n- **QoS hierarchy enabled**: if `--cgroups-per-qos=true` (line 398)\n- **CPU policy**: none, static, distribution-specific (line 328)\n- **Memory policy**: none, static, best-effort (line 344)\n- **Topology scope**: pod or container (line 299)\n\n### Start Flow (container_manager_linux.go:615-710)\n\nCalled by kubelet during sync loop initialization:\n\n```\nStart(ctx, node, activePods, getNode, sourcesReady, podStatusProvider, runtimeService, localStorageCapacityIsolation)\n├─ buildContainerMapAndRunningSetFromRuntime()    # Query current running containers via CRI\n├─ draManager.Start()                              # Initialize DRA resource claims\n├─ cpuManager.Start()                              # Load CPU allocation state from disk\n│   └─ reconcileState()                            # Periodically reconcile CPU allocations with cgroups\n├─ memoryManager.Start()                           # Load memory affinity state from disk\n├─ validateNodeAllocatable()                       # Verify capacity > allocatable\n├─ setupNode()                                     # Create cgroup hierarchy and system containers\n└─ deviceManager.Start()                           # Begin watching device plugins\n```\n\n**setupNode Details** (lines 504-584):\n1. Validate CPU hardcapping available (CPU CFS quota support)\n2. Setup kernel tunables if `--protect-kernel-defaults` not set\n3. Create node allocatable cgroup root: `kubepods/kubepods.slice`\n4. Start QoS container manager to create top-level QoS containers\n5. Enforce node allocatable limits by setting cgroup max resources\n6. Create system and kubelet cgroups for non-containerized processes\n7. Spawn background goroutines for periodic tasks (ensure state functions, QoS updates)\n\n### Pod Admission Flow\n\nContainer manager participates via topology manager returned by `GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()`:\n\n```\nkubelet AdmitPod()\n├─ topologyManager.Admit()                         # Collect topology hints\n│   ├─ cpuManager.GetTopologyHints()               # Preferred NUMA nodes for CPU\n│   ├─ memoryManager.GetTopologyHints()            # Preferred NUMA nodes for memory\n│   └─ deviceManager.GetTopologyHints()            # Device topology\n├─ deviceManager.Allocate()                        # Reserve device IDs\n├─ draManager.Prepare()                            # Claim resources from drivers\n└─ [admit decision: ALLOW / DENY]\n```\n\nIf admission succeeds:\n- CPU manager reserves exclusive CPUs\n- Device manager marks device IDs as allocated\n- DRA manager stores resource claims in state\n\n### Pod Sync Flow\n\nFor each pod reaching `Sync` phase:\n\n```\nkubelet SyncPod()\n├─ podContainerManager.EnsureExists()              # Create pod-level cgroup if needed\n├─ [for each container]\n│   ├─ internalContainerLifecycle.PreCreateContainer()\n│   │   ├─ cpuManager.GetCPUAffinity()             # Retrieve exclusive CPUs\n│   │   └─ memoryManager.GetMemoryNUMANodes()      # Retrieve NUMA affinity\n│   ├─ CreateContainer(containerConfig)            # CRI call with cpuset_cpus, cpuset_mems\n│   └─ internalContainerLifecycle.PreStartContainer()\n│       ├─ cpuManager.AddContainer()               # Record containerID mapping\n│       └─ memoryManager.AddContainer()            # Record containerID mapping\n└─ [periodic reconciliation]\n    └─ cpuManager reconcileState()                 # Verify cpuset_cpus matches cgroups\n        └─ runtimeService.UpdateContainerResources() # Correct drift\n```\n\n### Periodic Maintenance\n\n**Every 1 minute** (line 695):\n- Execute `ensureStateFunc` for system/kubelet cgroups to maintain process placement\n- Call `qosContainerManager.UpdateCgroups()` to rebalance QoS limits\n\n**Every 5 minutes** (line 695):\n- CPU manager reconciliation (if enabled)\n- Execute custom periodic tasks\n\n## Cgroup Hierarchy and Resource Enforcement\n\n### Hierarchy Structure (Linux with --cgroups-per-qos)\n\n```\n├─ systemd driver: /\n│  └─ kubepods.slice\n│     ├─ kubepods-kubepods.slice (node allocatable enforcement)\n│     │  ├─ kubepods-kubepods-burstable.slice (QoS class)\n│     │  │  └─ kubepods-kubepods-burstable-pod<uid>.slice (pod)\n│     │  │     ├─ container-id (container)\n│     │  │     └─ container-id\n│     │  └─ kubepods-kubepods-besteffort.slice (QoS class)\n│     │     └─ kubepods-kubepods-besteffort-pod<uid>.slice (pod)\n│     └─ (guaranteed pods placed directly in kubepods.slice/pod<uid>/)\n│\n├─ cgroupfs driver: /kubepods/\n│  ├─ kubepods (node allocatable enforcement)\n│  │  ├─ burstable/ (QoS class)\n│  │  │  └─ pod<uid>/ (pod)\n│  │  │     ├─ container-id (container)\n│  │  │     └─ container-id\n│  │  └─ besteffort/ (QoS class)\n│  │     └─ pod<uid>/ (pod)\n│  └─ (guaranteed pods in kubepods/pod<uid>/)\n```\n\n### Resource Parameter Mapping\n\n**CPU Limits** (cpu_manager.go):\n- Exclusive allocation: `cpu.cpuset` to specific cores\n- Shared allocation: `cpu.shares` (relative weight) + optional `cpu.cfs_quota_us`\n- Static policy: sum of exclusive allocations subtracted from shared pool\n\n**Memory Limits** (memory_manager.go):\n- Cgroup v1: `memory.limit_in_bytes` enforced hard limit\n- Cgroup v2:\n  - `memory.max`: hard limit (OOM at exceeding)\n  - `memory.min`: soft guarantee (will not reclaim below this)\n  - `memory.high`: throttle point (memcg pressure before OOM)\n- NUMA affinity: `cpuset.mems` (not a limit, but advisory for page allocation)\n\n**PID Limits** (types.go:41):\n- `pids.max`: maximum number of processes in cgroup\n- Pod-level: `--pod-pids-limit` flag (default 4096 on cgroup v2, unlimited on v1)\n\n## Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### Failure Mode: Cgroup Hierarchy Not Available\n\n**Symptom**: `\"failed to get mounted cgroup subsystems\"`\n\n**Root Cause**: Required cgroup subsystems not mounted in `/sys/fs/cgroup`\n\n**Handling** (lines 210-213):\n- Container manager init fails, kubelet enters crash loop\n- No graceful degradation; cgroups are mandatory for proper resource isolation\n\n**Mitigation**: Ensure cgroup controllers mounted before kubelet starts (typically systemd handles this)\n\n### Failure Mode: CPU Hardcapping Unavailable\n\n**Symptom**: `\"CPU hardcapping unsupported\"` (soft requirement, stored in Status.SoftRequirements)\n\n**Root Cause**: CPU CFS period/quota files missing (older kernel < 2.6.24 or cgroup v1 broken)\n\n**Handling** (lines 511-513):\n- Kubelet continues but cannot enforce CPU limits\n- CPU shares still applied, but no hard quota ceiling\n- Scheduled workloads may oversubscribe CPU\n\n**Mitigation**: Use kernel 5.x+ with full cgroup support enabled\n\n### Failure Mode: Cgroup Root Path Invalid\n\n**Symptom**: `\"invalid configuration: cgroups-per-qos was specified and cgroup-root was not specified\"`\n\n**Root Cause**: `--cgroup-root` flag missing when `--cgroups-per-qos=true`\n\n**Handling** (lines 260-261):\n- Init fails; kubelet cannot place pods\n- Required configuration: must specify `--cgroup-root=/kubepods` (or similar)\n\n### Failure Mode: Device Plugin Allocation Stale\n\n**Symptom**: Container fails to start; device IDs don't correspond to actual devices\n\n**Root Cause**: Device plugin crashed; allocation state became inconsistent\n\n**Handling** (devicemanager):\n- Pre-allocated device IDs may not exist at container start\n- Kubelet calls plugin's `GetDeviceRunContainerOptions()` which validates availability\n- If plugin returns error or device unavailable, container creation fails\n- Pod enters failed state; eviction or manual recovery required\n\n**Mitigation**: Device plugins must be highly available; use checkpoint/recovery\n\n### Failure Mode: Resource Reservation Exceeds Capacity\n\n**Symptom**: `\"node allocatable is negative\"`\n\n**Root Cause**: `--system-reserved` + `--kube-reserved` > node capacity\n\n**Handling** (kubelet node init, called by container manager):\n- Validation fails; kubelet cannot start\n- Must reconfigure reservations to be less than total capacity\n\n### Limit: Maximum Concurrent Resource Allocations\n\n**Constraint**: Single goroutine in `reconcileState()` per resource manager\n\n**Impact**: CPU and memory manager reconciliation runs serially, not in parallel\n\n**Rationale**: Simplifies state consistency; avoids thundering herd on runtime updates\n\n**Mitigation**: None; inherent architectural choice for safety\n\n### Limit: Cgroup Name Length\n\n**Constraint**: 64-character limit for cgroup filesystem names (kernel limit)\n\n**Impact**: Very long pod UIDs or container names may exceed directory name limits\n\n**Workaround**: Pod UID encoded as `pod<hash>` to reduce length\n\n### Design Tradeoff: State Persistence\n\n**Choice**: Separate state files per resource manager (cpumanager, memorymanager)\n\n**Tradeoff**:\n- ✅ Fault isolation: device manager crash doesn't corrupt CPU state\n- ✅ Per-manager recovery: CPU manager can replay allocations independently\n- ❌ Distributed consistency: no global transaction across all managers\n- ❌ Orphaned state: if pod UID reused, old allocations may not be cleaned (unlikely but possible)\n\n**Consistency Mechanism**: Active pod reconciliation on startup\n- Container manager queries runtime for all running containers\n- Pod controller ensures stale pod objects deleted before UIDs reused\n- Kubelet sync loop continuously validates pod ↔ cgroup mappings\n\n### Design Tradeoff: QoS Limits Soft-Enforced\n\n**Choice**: QoS container limits updated every 1 minute, not real-time\n\n**Rationale** (qos_container_manager_linux.go:45):\n- Real-time updates on pod add/remove would cause cgroup churn\n- 1-minute delay acceptable for capacity planning\n- Individual pod limits still enforced immediately via pod cgroups\n\n**Implication**: Transient oversubscription possible for up to 1 minute after pod deletion\n\n### Design Tradeoff: Topology Manager as Pod Admit Handler\n\n**Choice**: Topology manager admits or denies pods based on NUMA alignment hints\n\n**Rationale**:\n- Single authoritative decision point for all resource managers\n- Prevents race conditions between CPU, memory, device allocation decisions\n- Container must be started if admitted; no subsequent allocation failures expected\n\n**Risk**: If topology manager approves but CPU/device managers later fail to deliver:\n- Container creation fails; pod enters failed state\n- Should not happen with proper implementation; indicates bug\n\n## Extension Points, Customization Hooks, and Associated Risks\n\n### 1. CPU Manager Policy (cpu_manager.go:30-50)\n\n**Extension Points**:\n- Policy interface: `Manager` with `Allocate()`, `AddContainer()`, `RemoveContainer()`\n- Pluggable policies: `none`, `static`, distribution-specific (e.g., Intel platform extensions)\n\n**Customization**:\n```go\n// Implement interface in pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/\ntype CustomPolicy struct {\n    // ...\n}\nfunc (p *CustomPolicy) Allocate(pod, container) error { ... }\n```\n\nRegister in `cpu_manager.go` `NewManager()` switch statement.\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Incorrect exclusive CPU reservation: CPUs allocated to multiple containers (security/isolation issue)\n- ❌ Unbounded latency in `Allocate()`: Blocks pod admission; slow policies starve cluster\n- ❌ Inconsistent state: Custom policy doesn't persist state to disk; allocations lost on kubelet restart\n\n### 2. Memory Manager Policy (memory_manager.go:30-50)\n\n**Extension Points**:\n- Policy interface: `Manager` with `Allocate()`, `GetMemoryNUMANodes()`\n- Pluggable policies: `none`, `static`, `best-effort`\n\n**Risk**: Similar to CPU policy; mismatch between pod memory limit and allocated NUMA nodes causes page allocation contention\n\n### 3. Topology Manager Policy (topologymanager/policy.go)\n\n**Extension Points**:\n- Hint aggregation policies: `none`, `best-effort`, `restricted`, `single-numa-node`\n- Custom hint providers: `HintProvider` interface\n\n**Customization**:\n```go\ntype CustomProvider struct{}\nfunc (p *CustomProvider) GetTopologyHints(pod, container) map[string][]TopologyHint { ... }\nfunc (p *CustomProvider) GetPodTopologyHints(pod) map[string][]TopologyHint { ... }\n\ncm.topologyManager.AddHintProvider(logger, customProvider)\n```\n\n**Risk**: ❌ Custom providers returning inconsistent hints can cause deadlock (all providers reject; admission fails despite available resources)\n\n### 4. Device Plugin Interface (devicemanager/plugin/)\n\n**Extension Point**: External device plugin binary communicating via Unix socket gRPC\n\n**Plugin Lifecycle**:\n1. Plugin registers itself via inotify watch on `/var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins/`\n2. Kubelet calls plugin's `ListAndWatch()` to get available devices\n3. On pod admission: `Allocate()` called to reserve devices\n4. At container start: `GetDeviceRunContainerOptions()` called for mounts/env vars\n5. On pod deletion: device IDs freed; no explicit plugin call\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Plugin crash: Allocated devices become inaccessible; pod hangs at container start\n- ❌ Plugin stale socket: Old plugin process left running; new plugin can't claim socket\n- ❌ Unbounded device allocation: Plugin allocates more than reported capacity; node oversubscribed\n- ❌ Health check bypass: Plugin reports healthy but devices fail; kubelet doesn't know to evict\n\n**Mitigation**:\n- Plugin checkpoint/recovery mechanism\n- Kubelet kubelet health checks plugins via `GetHealthStatus()`\n- Device plugin spec forbids over-allocation\n\n### 5. Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA) Manager (dra/)\n\n**Extension Point**: External resource driver plugin with similar lifecycle to device plugins\n\n**Customization**:\n- Drivers implement `DynamicResourceAllocation` controller interface\n- Kubelet DRA manager calls driver via gRPC\n- Resource claims stored in cluster; recovered on pod restart\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Driver unavailable: Pending claims never provisioned; pod waits indefinitely\n- ❌ Claim conflicts: Two pods claim same resource; inconsistent state\n- ❌ Resource leak: Claim orphaned if cluster API unreachable; resource wasted\n\n**Mitigation**:\n- Claim state reconciliation against cluster state\n- Timeout-based claim cleanup\n\n### 6. Cgroup Driver Selection (cgroup_manager_linux.go:255)\n\n**Extension Point**: `--cgroup-driver` kubelet flag: `cgroupfs` or `systemd`\n\n**Customization**: Unlikely to extend; choice is platform-dependent\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Driver mismatch: Kubelet uses cgroupfs but container runtime uses systemd; cgroup limits don't apply\n- ❌ Name conversion bugs: Systemd cgroup path has invalid characters; cgroup not created\n\n### 7. QoS Reserved Configuration (container_manager.go:189)\n\n**Extension Point**: `--qos-reserved` kubelet flag (e.g., `memory=5%`)\n\n**Customization**: Percentage of memory reserved for memory QoS enforcement (cgroup v2 only)\n\n**Risk**: ❌ Over-reservation: QoS reserved memory wasted; pods can't use capacity\n- ❌ Under-reservation: Memory QoS enforcement fails; burstable pods get OOMed\n\n### 8. Node Allocatable Configuration\n\n**Extension Point**: `--system-reserved`, `--kube-reserved`, `--enforce-node-allocatable` flags\n\n**Customization**:\n```\n--system-reserved=cpu=100m,memory=512Mi,ephemeral-storage=1Gi\n--kube-reserved=cpu=100m,memory=100Mi\n--enforce-node-allocatable=pods,kube-reserved,system-reserved\n```\n\n**Associated Risks**:\n- ❌ Insufficient reservation: System OOMs or crashes due to pod memory pressure\n- ❌ Excessive reservation: Cluster capacity wasted; scheduler creates pod that can't fit in allocatable\n\n## Operational Implications\n\n### Performance Considerations\n\n**CPU Impact**:\n- Static CPU policy: O(n) CPU mask operations on pod add/remove (n = number of CPUs)\n- Topology manager: O(n*m) hint aggregation (n = managers, m = pods per node)\n- Cgroup updates: ~1ms per cgroup on cgroupfs; ~10ms via systemd D-Bus\n\n**Memory Impact**:\n- Container manager holds ~1KB per pod + per-container state\n- CPU manager state file grows with pod count (O(n) JSON objects)\n- Large pod counts (>1000 pods/node) require periodic state file compaction\n\n**Disk I/O**:\n- State persistence: CPU/memory managers write to `/var/lib/kubelet/` every allocation\n- Frequent pod churn (e.g., batch jobs) causes high I/O; consider SSD for kubelet dir\n\n### Reliability Considerations\n\n**Pod Reliability**:\n- Guaranteed pods: CPU and memory limits enforced; no eviction due to overload\n- Burstable pods: Soft limits; throttled but not OOMed unless node-level memory pressure\n- Best-effort pods: No resource guarantees; first to be evicted if node overloaded\n\n**Node Reliability**:\n- Cgroup enforcement essential for kubelet/system stability\n- Failure to enforce leads to system OOM; node becomes unresponsive\n- Device plugin crashes: Critical if pods depend on devices; manual intervention required\n- DRA resource driver: Failure blocks new pod admission until recovered\n\n**Graceful Degradation**:\n- CPU hardcapping unavailable: Kubelet continues but won't enforce quotas\n- Device plugin unavailable: New device allocations blocked; existing pods unaffected\n- DRA driver unavailable: Pending resource claims never provisioned; pod waits\n\n### Observability\n\n**Key Metrics** (pkg/kubelet/metrics/):\n- `kubelet_node_config_*`: Node capacity, allocatable, reserved\n- `container_*`: cAdvisor metrics for CPU, memory, I/O per container\n- `kubelet_pod_resource_status`: Allocated resources per pod (gRPC API)\n\n**Debugging**:\n- Check pod cgroup path: `kubelet logs | grep \"pod.*cgroup\"`\n- Query pod cgroup limits: `cat /sys/fs/cgroup/pods/pod<uid>/cpu.max`\n- Trace resource admission: Enable `kube-apiserver --vmodule=admission=4`\n- Monitor device plugin: Check `/var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins/<plugin>.sock`\n\n**Alerts**:\n- Node allocatable exceeded: CPU or memory reservation math wrong\n- Cgroup creation failures: Filesystem full or permission denied\n- Device allocation stale: Device plugin crashed; manual recovery needed\n- QoS limit churn: Frequent updates indicate high pod churn; consider batch job consolidation\n\n### Maintenance and Upgrades\n\n**State Cleanup**:\n- Stale state files: `/var/lib/kubelet/cpu_manager_state` removed on `--force-reset` flags\n- Orphaned cgroups: Manual cleanup if containers crash; `cgdelete` utility required\n\n**Cgroup Version Transitions**:\n- Upgrade from v1 to v2: Requires kernel with unified hierarchy support (5.2+)\n- No automatic migration; pods must be drained before cgroup root remount\n- Different resource parameter names (e.g., `cpu.weight` in v2 vs `cpu.shares` in v1)\n\n**Device Plugin Updates**:\n- Plugins should be deployed via DaemonSet with graceful shutdown hooks\n- Old plugin socket cleaned up before new plugin registered\n\n## Important Files and Directory Map\n\n### Core Container Manager\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go` | Core `ContainerManager` interface and constants |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go` | Main Linux implementation; initialization and orchestration |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_stub.go` | Stub implementation for unsupported platforms |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go` | ResourceConfig, CgroupName, NodeConfig type definitions |\n\n### Cgroup Management\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/cgroup_manager_linux.go` | CgroupManager interface and driver selection |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/cgroup_v1_manager_linux.go` | Cgroup v1 specific implementations |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/cgroup_v2_manager_linux.go` | Cgroup v2 specific implementations |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/helpers_linux.go` | Cgroup path helpers, resource calculation |\n\n### QoS and Pod Management\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/qos_container_manager_linux.go` | QoS hierarchy creation and periodic limit updates |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/pod_container_manager_linux.go` | Pod-level cgroup management |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/node_container_manager_linux.go` | Node allocatable and system container setup |\n\n### Container Lifecycle\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle.go` | Hook interface for CPU/memory affinity setup |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle_linux.go` | Linux implementation of lifecycle hooks |\n\n### Resource Managers\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/cpu_manager.go` | CPU allocation and affinity |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/policy_static.go` | Static CPU policy (exclusive allocation) |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/state/` | CPU allocation state persistence |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager/memory_manager.go` | Memory affinity and NUMA binding |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager/policy_static.go` | Static memory policy (NUMA pinning) |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/policy.go` | Topology-aware hint aggregation |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager/manager.go` | Device plugin lifecycle and allocation |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/dra/manager.go` | Dynamic Resource Allocation coordination |\n\n### Utilities and Testing\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/containermap/` | Container ID ↔ pod/container name mapping |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/util/` | Helper functions for resource calculations |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/fake_container_manager.go` | Mock implementation for testing |\n| `pkg/kubelet/cm/testing/` | Test utilities and fixtures |\n\n### Related Components (Outside cm/)\n\n| Path | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `pkg/kubelet/kubelet.go` | Kubelet main loop; invokes container manager |\n| `pkg/kubelet/eviction/` | Memory pressure eviction; uses container manager capacity |\n| `pkg/kubelet/lifecycle/` | Pod admission handlers; topology manager participates |\n| `pkg/kubelet/container/` | CRI abstraction; calls to update container resources |\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Kubelet Container Manager subsystem provides a unified abstraction for resource isolation and allocation across multiple dimensions: CPU exclusivity, memory NUMA affinity, device plugins, and dynamic resource drivers. By coordinating five semi-independent resource managers through a topology-aware admission handler, it enforces node allocatable constraints while supporting sophisticated allocation strategies.\n\nThe subsystem is well-architected for Linux cgroups but exhibits tight coupling to cgroup mechanics; porting to other isolation mechanisms (e.g., Windows Job Objects) requires significant reengineering. State management across managers is eventually consistent, relying on periodic reconciliation rather than global transactions. Operational success depends heavily on proper cgroup configuration, timely device plugin recovery, and correct reservation math to prevent node-level resource starvation.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
